# Lazio: law history in git

Part of [lexifina/global-law](https://github.com/lexifina/global-law), one git history for every country's law: this is the law of Lazio, Italy, which global-law reads at `italy/lazio/acts/`.

The law of the region of Lazio (Regione Lazio, Italy) as a git history: one file per article, one commit per change, dated the day it took effect. `git blame` names the law behind every paragraph; `git log` lists every change to an article; checking out a date gives the text in force that day (here: the text in force when it was retrieved).

It follows the shared design of [lexifina/global-law](https://github.com/lexifina/global-law), which places it at `it-62/`. Italy's national law is in [lexifina/italy-law](https://github.com/lexifina/italy-law).

The text is in Italian, exactly as the source prints it.

## What this repository holds

1908 acts with 1908 versions so far: the Statute (Statuto regionale) first, then the regional laws, added in parts of about fifty. Every regional law the database lists is included except those it marks as repealed as a whole or whose text it omits ("Omissis"); provisions repealed later are printed in square brackets, as the source prints them. Regulations are not included yet.

## Source

The regional-law database of the Consiglio regionale del Lazio, <https://www.consiglio.regione.lazio.it/?vw=leggiregionali>, "Leggi regionali (testo coordinato)": one page per regional law with its coordinated text and the editor's notes naming the law behind each change. The Statute (legge statutaria 11 novembre 2004, n. 1) is taken from the Consiglio's PDF of its consolidated text (October 2013, with the amendments of 2012 and 2013) on the Statute page, reading the PDF's text layer, one paragraph per line. Laws whose whole text the database marks as repealed are left out.

## Dates

- Each act's text is the consolidated text in force on the retrieval day (30 October 2026), as the Consiglio regionale del Lazio publishes it. The source gives no dated past versions (its "testo storico" is the text as first published, with no dated intermediate versions), so each act has one commit, its **oldest version available**, dated at **noon, Europe/Rome, on 30 October 2026** and credited to the Consiglio regionale del Lazio as publisher, naming the act itself.
- This is not the date the text took effect: it is the day the text was read. Earlier wordings are not in this record.
- Versions that take effect after the retrieval day are on the `futuro` branch, never merged into `main`.

## Files

`statuto/art-001.md` is Articolo 1 of the Statute; `lr-2004-008/art-005.md` is Articolo 5 of legge regionale 3 agosto 2004, n. 8. `intestazione.md` holds what the page prints before the first article (title, summary, preamble); `note.md` the editor's notes ("Note:") that the page prints after the text. Tables are one cell per line. One paragraph per line.

## How it is checked

- Every version of every act is read back from git on the day it took effect and on the day before (`tools/check_repo.py`); both must match `corpus.json`.
- The letters and digits of every version equal those of the Consiglio regionale del Lazio's own text of that version (read by a second, independent reader), and every unit's text appears in it in order.
- Health checks: no merge commits, commit dates never go backwards, nothing dated in the future, and every commit's trailers parse.

## What "author" means

Every commit here is an "oldest version available" commit, authored by the publisher, the Consiglio regionale del Lazio, with an e-mail address that cannot exist. The committer is always Lexifina.

## Limits

This is not an official publication. The Consiglio regionale del Lazio states on every page: "Il testo non ha valore legale; rimane, dunque, inalterata l'efficacia degli atti legislativi originari." The texts with legal value are those published in the Bollettino ufficiale della Regione Lazio. Only the current text of each act is recorded, not its earlier wordings. Only the acts listed above are included.
